# C88: complete elimination of the curved partial-tail chronology

Status: a complete, self-contained analytical proof DRAFT, NOT a verified fact.
This is the single recovery round authorized by TASK.md and shared guidance
6c702a3eacae802d on 2026-09-30. Numerical/model/word discovery and fact_submit
remain closed. No computation of a candidate word is used in this proof.

The result below resolves the new family proposed in 657b4a00036ec84f. In
fact, it allows every lower clipping parameter that preserves its chronology,
not just the parameter that puts the third query at the lower ramp boundary.
The entire family adds NO constraint to the enlarged unit-curvature system.
This is a redundancy result for an explicitly specified family, not a
statement about all one-dimensional functions or the original seed cone.

## 1. Statement, canonical variables, and scope

Write the actual four steps as

    h=(u,v,w,z)=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),
    H=u+v, R=w+z, s=H+R, C=1+s, P=u+1.

For this proof assume the following deliberately coarse open box:

    1<u,z<9/5, 1<v,w<5/2, C<9.                       (1)

This contains the canonical box specified in the complete evidence of
81720922d2d132d7 and used in 7cd0bb4a795ee1df:

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97,
    0<b,c<1552559/1081453,
    C=5+a+b+c+d.                                     (2)

Indeed 77/97<4/5 and 1552559/1081453<3/2, by direct cross multiplication.
The proof here takes (1) as an explicit hypothesis; it does not promote the
unverified compact-domain derivation to a fact. Thus it applies, without
any dependency on that derivation, to every actual parameter in the joint
canonical region (2), whatever further constraints are imposed there.
In particular C is variable; it is never fixed to 193/25.

For reciprocal curvature t and lower clipping magnitude alpha, let

    1<=t<v, B=v-t>0,
    0<=alpha<=alpha_B(t):=B/(w+t),
    f_(t,alpha)(x)=x-t/2                   if x>=t,
                  x^2/(2t)               if -t*alpha<=x<=t,
                  -alpha*x-t*alpha^2/2   if x<=-t*alpha.
                                                               (3)

The formulas agree at both boundaries. Start actual gradient descent at
x0=u+t and use the original four steps h. The curvature on the middle
segment is k=1/t, strictly between 1/v and 1 inclusive. This is the whole
partial-tail chronology: the first two gradients are the upper cap, and
the next two are the lower cap. The terminal gradient may leave that cap.
At the endpoint alpha=alpha_B, its expression in curvature k is
(k*v-1)/(1+k*w), exactly the parameter in plan 657b4a00036ec84f.

Let V be the ORIGINAL primitive energy of this actual trajectory:
write f_i=f_(t,alpha)(x_i) and g_i=f'_(t,alpha)(x_i), for 0<=i<=4.

    V=2 sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f4)
       +sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g4^2.       (4)

Then the following are equivalent on (1):

    (i) V>=0 for every t and alpha in (3);

    (ii) C(v-1) <= (u+1)(1+w+z),
         C(v-1)(z-1) <= (u+1)(1+w).                  (5)

In canonical variables these are exactly

    C b <= (2+a)(3+c+d),
    C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                             (6)

Both are already in the enlarged unit-curvature system. Thus adding ALL
the genuine curvatures and ALL the lower clips in this chronology leaves
that entire joint region unchanged. This statement is not just an
equivalent quantified reformulation: the two continuous parameters have
been removed, and (6) is an already present finite list.

No order between u,v,w,z is assumed. There is no cut saturation,
quadratic saturation, primitive-prefix assumption, symmetry, or fixed
capacity. In particular no claim of nonemptiness of the enlarged joint
region is made.

## 2. One static legal function and every interpolation pair

The derivative of (3) is clamp(x/t,[-alpha,1]). It is continuous,
nondecreasing, and 1/t-Lipschitz, hence f is convex, differentiable, and
1-smooth. For alpha>0 its unique minimizer is 0, with value 0. At alpha=0
its minimizer set is the negative half-line including 0, which is allowed.
The starting point x0=u+t is positive in either case. No jets from
different functions are combined.

The actual queries, gradients, and terminal coordinate are

    x0=u+t,       g0=1,
    x1=t,         g1=1,
    x2=-B,        g2=-alpha,
    x3=-B+w*alpha,g3=-alpha,
    x4=-B+R*alpha,
    g4=clamp(x4/t,[-alpha,1]).                        (7)

To justify both lower-cap claims, alpha<=B/(w+t) gives
B>=(w+t)alpha>=t alpha, so x2<=-t alpha. It also gives
x3=-B+w alpha<=-t alpha. Every displayed update is therefore exactly
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i for the single function (3).

At every sample, including a clipping boundary,

    f_i=x_i*g_i-t*g_i^2/2.                           (8)

For a direct audit of ALL interpolation pairs, put n_i=x_i-t*g_i.
If g_i is the upper endpoint 1, then n_i>=0; if it is the lower
endpoint -alpha, then n_i<=0; otherwise n_i=0. Thus, for any two
sample indices i,j,

    n_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.

The original signed residual consequently satisfies

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =n_j*(g_i-g_j)-(t-1)*(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.        (9)

This includes every distinct ordered pair in {0,1,2,3,4}, and also every
pair involving the optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0, for which n_star=0.
When alpha=0 the normal assertion still holds: the lower endpoint is 0
and its normal is nonpositive. No formal coordinate or multiplier is
deleted merely because some actual gradients in (7) coincide.

For interpretation only, accepted fact 04219554e41d9750, Section 10,
gives the following full original-space identity. Let e be the five-entry
all-ones column, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and zero for t>i, and retain
all twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j, with Lambda_ii=0. Put

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2v,-2w,-2z,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(u(2-u),v(2-v),w(2-w),z(2-z),-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2.

If L_flow^T e=omega, then

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g0,...,g4)>.
                                                               (10)

Here Gram contains all five formal gradients. A full PSD certificate
therefore implies V>=0 on (7). Conversely a negative V from this family
rules out that full certificate. No converse from V>=0 to a certificate
is asserted. Formula (9) proves actual all-pair legality directly, in the
same orientation as accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290.

## 3. Exact energy, retaining the curvature deficit

Telescoping x_i^2-x_(i+1)^2=2h_i*x_i*g_i-h_i^2*g_i^2,
using (8), gives

    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2
       =x0^2-x4^2-(t-1)sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2.

Substitute the actual f4 from (8) into (4), and complete the square:

    V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2
       -(t-1)[sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2-s*g4^2].            (11)

The bracketed term cannot be discarded by pretending the values have
unit-curvature saturation. In (7), sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i^2=H+R*alpha^2.
Define

    E0(t)=(u+t)^2-(t-1)H,
    q(t)=1+s/t+s*C/t^2,
    D(t)=t^2+s*t+s*C=t^2*q(t).

Useful elementary identities are

    E0(1)=P^2,
    E0(t)=P^2+(t-1)(t+1+u-v),
    E0'(t)=2t+u-v>1/2 for 1<=t<=v,
    q(1)=C^2, q'(t)=-s/t^2-2s*C/t^3<0.             (12)

Thus E0(t)>=P^2>4, with strict increase for t>1.

## 4. Eliminate every terminal clipping branch and alpha

First the terminal upper cap is IMPOSSIBLE on the stated domain; it is
not suppressed as an assumption. As alpha increases, x4=-B+R alpha
increases. Its maximum is reached at alpha_B, where

    x4=alpha_B*(z-t).

If z<=t it is nonpositive. If z>t, then

    x4/t <= alpha_B*(z-1)
          <=[(v-1)/(w+1)]*(z-1)
          <(3/4)*(4/5)=3/5<1.                       (13)

Consequently no value of alpha in (3), at any curvature, can reach the
upper terminal cap or its boundary. For completeness, without this box
the upper-branch formula from (11) would be

    V=E0(t)-(x4+s)^2-(t-1)R alpha^2+(t-1)s,

but its domain x4>=t has empty intersection with (3) under (1).

The lower/core terminal boundary is the positive number

    alpha_A(t)=B/(R+t),
    0<alpha_A<alpha_B,                               (14)

because R=w+z>w. On 0<=alpha<=alpha_A, x4<=-t alpha and
g4=-alpha. Formula (11) becomes

    V=E0(t)-(B+H alpha)^2+(t-1)H alpha^2
      =E0(t)-B^2-2BH alpha-H(H+1-t)alpha^2.          (15)

Since H+1-t=u+1+B>0 and B,H>0, (15) strictly decreases with alpha.
Its minimum on this whole branch is at alpha_A. At alpha=0 it is

    V=H*(u+1+t-v)>0,

because u+1+t-v>=u+2-v>1/2.

On alpha_A<=alpha<=alpha_B, the terminal gradient is x4/t and

    V=E0(t)-(t-1)R alpha^2-q(t)(R alpha-B)^2.         (16)

This is a concave quadratic in alpha, since q(t)>0 and R>0.
For any point of the interval the defining concavity inequality bounds
its value below by the linear interpolation of its endpoint values;
therefore its minimum is at one of the two endpoints. Formulas (15)
and (16) agree at alpha_A because g4=-alpha_A there. They also remain
valid at alpha_B, where x3=-t alpha_B is exactly the boundary between
the lower flat segment and the middle ramp.

We have now proved, without any division by an energy or a residual,
that at each fixed t the WHOLE clipping interval is nonnegative if
and only if both endpoint energies V_A(t), V_B(t) are nonnegative.

## 5. Eliminate curvature at the full-tail endpoint

At alpha=alpha_A, x4=-t alpha_A and g4=-alpha_A. Formula (11) gives

    V_A(t)=E0(t)-[(t+s)alpha_A]^2
                     +(t-1)H alpha_A^2.            (17)

The nonnegative quantity in square brackets is

    J(t)=(v-t)(t+s)/(R+t)
        =(v-t)*(1+H/(R+t)).

It decreases on [1,v], because

    J'(t)=-(1+H/(R+t))-H(v-t)/(R+t)^2<0.

Using (12) and the nonnegative last term in (17) yields

    V_A(t)>=V_A(1)
          =P^2-[C(v-1)/(R+1)]^2.                   (18)

The first inequality in (5) is therefore necessary and sufficient for
every full-tail endpoint curvature. For t>1, E0(t)>E0(1) shows strict
positivity whenever V_A(1)>=0. No small-curvature approximation is used.

## 6. Eliminate curvature at the partial-tail return endpoint

At alpha=alpha_B write alpha(t)=(v-t)/(w+t). Then

    x3=-t alpha(t), x4=(z-t)alpha(t),
    g4=(z-t)alpha(t)/t,
    V_B(t)=E0(t)-(t-1)R alpha(t)^2
                    -q(t)alpha(t)^2(z-t)^2.         (19)

At t=1 this is

    V_B(1)=P^2-[C(v-1)(z-1)/(w+1)]^2.               (20)

Thus necessity of the second inequality in (5) follows by an actual
unit-curvature member, not by a limiting construction.

For sufficiency suppose V_B(1)>=0. We must keep BOTH signs of z-t.

### 6a. Return to the positive ray: 1<=t<=z

Put d=z-1 in (0,4/5) and r=t-1 in [0,d], also retaining t<v.
The ratio alpha(t) decreases with t and q(t)<=q(1)=C^2. Therefore

    q(t)alpha(t)^2(z-t)^2
       <=[P^2-V_B(1)]*(d-r)^2/d^2.

Using the exact E0 in (12), (19) consequently gives

    V_B(t)>=V_B(1)*(d-r)^2/d^2
       +r*[2+u-v+r+P^2*(2/d-r/d^2)-R alpha(t)^2].   (21)

The bracket is uniformly positive over the entire box (1). Explicitly,

    2+u-v+r>1/2,
    P^2*(2/d-r/d^2)>=P^2/d>5,
    R alpha(t)^2<(9/2)*(3/4)^2=81/32.

The first estimate uses u>1,v<5/2. The second uses r<=d,
P>2,d<4/5. The third uses R=w+z<9/2 and
alpha(t)<=alpha(1)<3/4. Hence the bracket exceeds 95/32. In particular,

    V_B(t)>=V_B(1)*((z-t)/(z-1))^2
                        +(95/32)*(t-1),            (22)

with strict inequality in the added estimate when t>1. It follows
that every t in this branch has nonnegative energy, strictly positive
when t>1. This includes t=z if that endpoint lies below v.

### 6b. Terminal point on the negative ray: z<=t<v

This branch has a direct positive margin, independent of (20).
Put r=t-1. Then 0<=r<v-1<3/2, and

    |g4|=alpha(t)*(t-z)/t
         <=r*(3/2-r)/[(1+r)(2+r)]<1/6.             (23)

For the strict last inequality, subtract the numerator after
cross multiplication:

    (1+r)(2+r)-6r*(3/2-r)
       =7r^2-6r+2=7(r-3/7)^2+5/7>0.

Moreover

    (t-1)alpha(t)^2
       <=r*(3/2-r)^2/4<=1/8.                       (24)

The last scalar bound follows by differentiating
r*(3/2-r)^2 on [0,3/2]: its only interior maximum occurs at r=1/2
and has value 1/2. This is a fixed symbolic inequality, not a schedule
or parameter scan.

Since t<5/2, s<8, and C<9,

    D(t)=t^2+s*t+s*C<25/4+20+72=393/4.

Equation (19), written as
V_B=E0-(t-1)R alpha^2-D(t)g4^2, together with (12), (23), and (24),
therefore proves the uniform bound

    V_B(t)>4-9/16-131/48=17/24>0.                  (25)

This covers every order between v and z: if z>=v the branch is empty;
if z<v it covers the entire remaining curvature interval.

Sections 6a and 6b exhaust [1,v). Thus (20)>=0 is sufficient for ALL
partial-tail endpoint curvatures. Together with Sections 4 and 5, this
proves the equivalence (5) for the WHOLE two-parameter family.

## 7. Boundary, zero-credit, and denominator audit

* t=1 is included exactly. The curvature-deficit term in (11) is then
  zero, but no step divides by t-1. Both constraints in (5) are attained
  by positive finite alpha values at t=1.
* alpha=0 is a valid static function with a nonunique minimizer. Its
  energy is the positive value given after (15); it is not discarded.
* At alpha_A the lower and middle formulas agree. At alpha_B query3
  lies exactly on the lower ramp boundary. Both are actual trajectories.
* Terminal upper clipping and its equality boundary were excluded by
  the strict bound (13), not by an unproved cap assumption. Lower
  terminal clipping was fully retained in (15).
* At t=z the terminal gradient is zero. Both (22) and (25) apply if
  t=z<v, and neither divides by g4 or z-t.
* The closure t=v forces B=alpha_A=alpha_B=alpha=0. The limiting actual
  trajectory is x=(u+v,v,0,0,0), g=(1,1,0,0,0), with curvature 1/v
  and V=H*(u+1)>0. The same formulas remain valid. Curvatures t>v
  cannot put query2 on the negative flat part with alpha>0; they are
  outside this specified chronology, not an omitted branch of it.
* All denominators t,w+t,R+t,w+1,R+1 are strictly positive. The only
  division by d=z-1 occurs in the positive-return branch with d>0.
  On the optional boundary z=1 that branch has only t=1; (19) at t=1
  is P^2, and the negative-ray argument handles t>1. No zero-denominator
  case is silently assigned a limit value in the proof.
* There is no cut-repair weight or division by an interpolation credit
  anywhere in this family. Vanishing residuals, repeated actual gradients,
  and zero curvature-deficit t-1 cause no singularity.
* Equality in either unit constraint is allowed. Its endpoint may have
  V=0 at t=1; every member with t>1 has strictly positive endpoint
  energies and hence positive energy throughout the clipping interval.

## 8. Direct screening, consequence, and remaining full interface

The direct route succeeds: monotonicity of (15), concavity of (16), and
the two curvature estimates completely remove alpha and t. The backup
cubic-stationary-root route is unnecessary. It is not an additional
result or a claim of computational discovery.

Every schedule in the enlarged canonical necessary system already
satisfies (6), so every such schedule survives the ENTIRE curved
partial-tail family (3). This remains true at every unit-constraint
equality and for every capacity in (2). Thus curvature in the selected
chronology cannot resolve any surviving joint region. The obstruction
is concrete: each interior alpha is controlled by two endpoint
energies; the full-tail endpoint is weakest at unit curvature; the
positive return loses terminal energy faster than it incurs the exact
curvature deficit; and a negative return has the uniform margin (25).

No new surviving schedule or interval is asserted. In particular the
old h(t) interval from 480b207356f9095f is already rejected by the
unit-return inequality and is not reconsidered here. This proof does
not imply that the enlarged necessary region is nonempty or empty.

The unresolved seed question remains: does any four-step schedule of
sum at least 168/25 admit ALL twenty nonnegative multipliers with the
exact flow and the full five-gradient PSD matrix in (10)? Passing this
family does not provide those multipliers. Accepted 04219554e41d9750
remains conditional on a seed that has not been constructed. The
original arbitrary-h asymptotic exact-profile problem remains open.

One genuinely different next geometry would use a two-dimensional
gradient set with noncollinear exposed faces and an anisotropic
quadratic conjugate, so the actual gradients on the successive return
need not remain parallel. A concrete starting representation is

    F(x)=sup_(g in K) [<x,g>-(1/2)<A g,g>],

where K is a compact convex polygon containing 0 and A is a symmetric
matrix with A>=I. The actual-query conditions are
x_i-A g_i in N_K(g_i), together with the original GD recurrences.
Here I is the identity matrix and
N_K(g)={n:<n,v-g><=0 for every v in K} is the normal cone.
This is a proposed new geometry, not a realized seed, certificate, or
authorized numerical search. The scalar endpoint/concavity argument
above must not be extended to it without a new proof. Another query
chronology, in which query3 has left the lower flat segment, is also
outside (3); no all-curvature claim about that chronology is made.

## 9. Provenance and resource accounting

This joint proof uses the accepted full-certificate identity
04219554e41d9750 and the consistent interpolation convention in
1252b8823bb5a290. Every new function, trajectory, pair residual, energy,
and parameter inequality used here is derived in this document.
The box and unit-constraint mapping are made explicit, so no shared
unverified claim is used as a hidden premise.

Matlas literature retrieval returned no applicable new result. The
already downloaded Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper, arXiv:1502.05666,
Theorem 4 and its proof, was reread for context. No theorem from it is
imported; (9) supplies the exact required all-pair calculation.

Round deltas: zero numerical/model/word evaluations, zero solver calls,
zero numerical replays, zero fact_submit calls, zero installations,
zero subagents. The earlier disclosed 120/96 word overrun remains
closed and is not reallocated or relabeled by this analytical result.
The interrupted earlier attempt remains separate from this one
completed recovery round. This document is UNVERIFIED until accepted
through a separately authorized verifier gate.

# C104: free positive endpoint angles remain below the original same-word reference

Status: FINAL JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Worker max3, allocation round2 under
master_guidance78e668c9782fc528. Completed baseline33; this return completes35
of ceiling35, attempted ceiling36 including the historical interruption.
Latest guidance read:1501c682ec2aff49, preserving this existing final round
and all zero formal/computational allowances.
No verification or computational resource is allocated or used. C103 is
awareness only; every new ingredient used together is proved in this document.

The original target remains OPEN: for every predetermined nonnegative
length-n schedule, establish the unrestricted lower bound
W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n uniformly as n tends to infinity, in the original W=2R
normalization. The results below are restrictions on an explicit adversarial
family, not upper bounds on unrestricted W_n and not target completion.

Accepted premises, within their scopes:1252b8823bb5a290 (normalization and
directed interpolation convention);b98bb807d514e80d (the complete K_n and
original all-split benchmark);1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete alternating-ray
reference);dfde2fa326c908bf (Huber-augmented universal-lens reference).
No C99/C100/C101/C102/C103 draft is an accepted premise.

## 1. Declared family and quantitative conclusion

Keep every physical query of the ORIGINAL word, partitioned as

    h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2),
    S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2,
    A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, sigma=2N-1.

Each block is its original possibly empty list of nonnegative steps.
The letters T,M,N,A,E below are local block/checkpoint abbreviations,
not replacements for the original benchmark B_n or its index.

Choose

    u>=0, v>0, alpha>=0, beta>=0, kappa>=0,
    s>=0, ell>0,
    delta=A-Mu-alpha, H=Mv+beta,
    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(s,ell), a_r=s^2+ell^2,
    z=(C,V)=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta).

The single static function and initial point are

    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},
    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2},
    X_0=(T+delta,-H).                                      (1)

Impose exactly the following support-plane activity conditions:

    delta>=0,
    vH<=(1+u)delta+kappa,
    kappa<=u alpha+v beta;                                 (2)

    (1-s)delta+ell H>=0,
    (1-s)(delta+S_0)+ell H>=0;                              (3)

    <r,z>-N a_r>=0,
    <r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r+su+ell v)>=0,
    <r-p,z>-(a_r-s)>=0,
    <r-p,z>-N(a_r-s)>=0.                                  (4)

There is NO cap s<=1 or prescribed final coordinate ratio. Both prefix
endpoints (3) and both tail endpoints of the p comparison (4) are retained.
Every feasible parameter tuple gives gradient p at all block_0 queries
and its checkpoint, q throughout block_1 and its checkpoint, and r at
every block_2 query including the actual terminal point.

The true closest minimizer, squared radius, and exact payoff are

    t_star=min(H,kappa/v), x_star=(0,-t_star),
    R104^2=(T+delta)^2+(H-kappa/v)_+^2,
    V104=2F(X_n)/R104^2
        =[2s(Eu-alpha)+2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma a_r]/R104^2.     (5)

Here positive part is t_+=max(t,0); R104 is an actual distance, unrelated
to the project symbol R in W=2R. For every word with T>=2A, we prove

    V104<=max{ (AE/(TM))^2/(2N-1),
               E^2/[(2N-1)(T+A+M)^2] }.                  (6)

For the ORIGINAL enclosing-m ruler, with the SAME parameters in all
its child words,

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,
    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a_m c_m^k,H_k),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, M_k=1+sum H_k,

every actual checkpoint pair obeys T>=2A when m>=128. We prove jointly

    sup_(all pairs and all (2)--(4)) V104
       <= gamma104 K_n(H_m)=gamma104 F_ray_n(H_m),
    gamma104=61473/68840<1,
    K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1),                      (7)

    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V104
       <=2gamma104/exp(1)<1.                              (8)

The complete applicable Huber-augmented lens reference also dominates.
This closes free angles and magnitudes throughout the stated positive
quadrant of final gradients, with independent middle coordinates and
intercepts. Negative first coordinate of r is a precise remaining escape;
Section11 gives actual static data there, without a profile-gain claim.

## 2. Static regularity, minimum and actual radius

J is finite, continuous, convex and nonnegative. Its proximal objective
is coercive and strictly convex, so for every x it has a unique minimizer
y(x). Comparing that minimizer with y+t(w-y), using convexity, dividing
by t>0 and letting t decrease to0 gives

    g(x):=x-y(x) in partial J(y(x)).

Conversely this subgradient condition, plus the quadratic expansion,
shows that any competitor w has proximal objective at least
F(x)+||w-y(x)||^2/2. The two subgradient inequalities at x,x' give
<g-g',y-y'>>=0 and hence ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Thus g is1-Lipschitz.
Evaluating the proximal objective for x' at y(x) gives its upper first
order bound. For the lower bound use the subgradient inequality at y(x)
and the quadratic expansion at y(x'). Together they give

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.

Consequently F is differentiable with gradient g and is convex. It is an
everywhere-defined convex1-smooth function, proved directly here.

Its minimum is0 and its minimizer set is precisely

    K={x_1<=0, s x_1+ell x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}.  (9)

The inclusion J=0 implies F=0. Conversely F=0 forces both nonnegative
terms in its attained proximal objective to vanish, hence x=y and J(x)=0.
Every point of K has x_1<=0 and x_2>=-kappa/v, since u>=0. The second
coordinate is NOT generally bounded above by0 when s>0; we do not
replace this tilted minimizer set by the orthogonal triangle.

The point x_star in (5) belongs to K, since its first coordinate is0
and its second coordinate lies in[-kappa/v,0]. Because X_0,1=T+delta>0,
the first coordinate distance to K is separately minimized at0. Because
X_0,2=-H<0, its second coordinate distance under the universal lower
bound -kappa/v is separately minimized at -t_star.
These two minima are simultaneously attained at x_star. This proves the
exact closest minimizer and both positive-part branches of (5).

## 3. Every physical query and every directed interpolation row

Assign the listed slopes p,q,r,0 the affine costs0,kappa,0,0. If g_i's
plane is active at y_i=x_i-g_i, the proximal optimality condition gives
the actual gradient and value

    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i.

For every ordered pair, including different blocks and remote queries,
the convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 yields

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                       (10)

The last inequality is precisely activity of j's plane against i's.
Include x_star with zero value, gradient and cost. Its zero plane is
active; therefore the SAME identity proves both optimum orientations.
No row is deleted, and (10) covers coincident queries and zero steps.

At prefix cumulative mass t in[0,S_0], the proposed proximal point is

    y=(delta+S_0-t,-H).

The p-versus-zero and p-versus-q comparisons are weakest at t=S_0 and
are exactly the first two inequalities of (2). Its comparison with r
is (1-s)(delta+S_0-t)+ell H>=0. It is affine in t, so (3) proves it
at every physical query for ALL s>=0, including s>1.

The checkpoint b sends the point to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle
cumulative mass t in[0,S_1], the proposed proximal point is

    y=(-alpha-(S_1-t)u,-beta-(S_1-t)v).

At t=S_1 the q-plane height is u alpha+v beta-kappa>=0, whereas
p's height is-alpha<=0 and r's is-s alpha-ell beta<=0. Earlier queries
add (S_1-t)q; the q-plane directional slope u^2+v^2 exceeds those of
zero,p,r, namely0,-u,-su-ell v. Thus every middle query is legal.

The checkpoint d sends the point to z. At tail cumulative mass t in
[0,S_2], put k=1+t; its proposed proximal point is y=z-k r, where
1<=k<=N. The r-versus-zero comparison decreases with slope a_r>0.
The r-versus-q comparison decreases with slope a_r+su+ell v>0.
Their ending values are the first two quantities in (4). The r-versus-p
comparison is <r-p,z>-k(a_r-s), which may either increase or decrease.
Its two actual endpoints in (4) prove legality throughout this block.
This gives the full supplied recursion and proves (10) jointly.

The family is nonempty for every physical partition under consideration.
For example set u=alpha=beta=kappa=0,
v=min{sqrt(A/(2M)),N/E}, ell=Ev/(2N), and s=ell/2. Then delta=A,
s<=1/4, Mv^2<=A/2, and the prefix/middle comparisons hold as written.
On the tail z=(0,Ev), the proximal coordinates satisfy y_1=-ks<0 and
y_2=Ev-k ell>=Ev/2>0. Thus p and q have negative heights, while
r's height is at least ell Ev-N(s^2+ell^2)=3N ell^2/4>0. This is
an actual acute member, already covered by the complete bound below.

These conditions are also necessary for the stated gradients of (1).
For a finite affine maximum a subgradient is a convex combination of
active slopes: otherwise separation from their finite convex hull
contradicts its directional derivative, the maximum active directional
slope. The slope q is uniquely exposed by direction(0,-1), and r by
(0,1), so their occurrence as gradients requires their planes active.
For p this argument need not use exposedness, which can fail for s>1.
If p is any convex combination of active slopes with weights w_j,
their common value is J(y)=<p,y>-sum w_j k_j<=<p,y>, since all costs
are nonnegative. The p-plane itself gives J(y)>=<p,y>, so it is active.
Applying these facts at the ending middle query and the prefix/tail
endpoints gives (2)--(4). Intermediate comparisons have just been proved.

At the actual terminal query the value formula is
2f_n=2<r,z>-sigma a_r, as in (5). The first tail condition gives
2f_n>=a_r>0. Multiplying F by any L>0 retains all points under the
original steps h_i/L and leaves the normalized payoff unchanged.

## 4. Distinct reserves, optimum slacks, and paid holding continuation

The ending-query affine reserves f_i-||g_i||^2/2 are, separately,

    delta, u alpha+v beta-kappa, L_0:=<r,z>-N a_r.

The optimum slack at a sample is

    <g_i,x_i-x_star>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2=k_i-<g_i,x_star>.

It equals0 for p, kappa-v t_star for q, and ell t_star for r. These
are nonnegative, but they are not the displayed affine reserves.

Define the three distinct terminal support-plane margins

    L_0=<r,z-Nr>,
    L_p=<r-p,z-Nr>,
    L_q=<r-q,z-Nr>+kappa.

All are nonnegative. For an appended list of nonnegative steps of total
mass tau, the SAME function keeps gradient r at EVERY appended query
exactly for 0<=tau<=T_hold, where

    T_hold=min{ L_0/a_r,
                L_q/(a_r+su+ell v),
                L_p/(a_r-s) if a_r>s, otherwise +infinity }. (11)

Indeed the proposed proximal point becomes z-(N+tau)r. Its three
comparisons are L_0-tau a_r, L_q-tau(a_r+su+ell v), and
L_p-tau(a_r-s). The last gives no upper limit when a_r<=s. Each
positive-slope comparison is weakest at the final partial mass; any
nonpositive-slope comparison improves. Exposedness of r gives necessity.
Every intermediate physical query and the true minimizer set remain
accounted for. During this exact holding range,

    V104(tau)=V104(0)-2tau a_r/R104^2.                    (12)

Positive reserve alone therefore gives neither unpriced holding nor a
claim after (11). A changed-gradient continuation needs separate analysis.

## 5. Valid reduction, with no unjustified homogeneous conversion

From (2) alone, cancellation of beta gives

    Mv^2<=(1+u)delta+u alpha=delta+u(A-Mu),
    R104^2>=R_0^2:=(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2.        (13)

The radius inequality follows from kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Completing
each terminal square, using s,ell>=0 and alpha,beta>=0, gives

    2s(Eu-alpha)+2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma(s^2+ell^2)
       <=2E(su+ell v)-sigma(s^2+ell^2)
       <=E^2(u^2+v^2)/sigma.

Since the true numerator is positive,

    V104<=E^2(u^2+v^2)/(sigma R_0^2).                   (14)

This is an upper relaxation. It neither replaces the actual static
function mid-trajectory nor assumes the square-maximizing r is legal.

For completeness, on the subdomain0<=s<=1 the change beta'=0,
kappa'=u alpha, keeping the other parameters and the word fixed, IS a
legal whole-instance improvement. The prefix r comparison remains
automatic. The new first q comparison is exactly (13); the new middle
reserve is0. At every tail query, z increases by(0,beta): the zero and
p margins rise by ell beta, and the q margin rises by
ell beta+(u alpha+v beta-kappa)>=0. Its radius does not increase and
its positive numerator rises by2ell beta. This legal conversion is
not asserted for s>1, where the prefix r comparison must also be paid.
The proof of (14) and all conclusions below do not need that conversion.

## 6. New full middle-gradient norm versus true-radius comparison

We prove from scratch: for A,M,T>0 with T>=2A, u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,

    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,
    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),

one has

    (u^2+v^2)/[(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2]
       <=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.                 (15)

This controls the horizontal contribution that the former orthogonal
argument did not need. It includes both nearest-minimizer branches.

First suppose M<=A+2A^2/T. Put P=A-Mu and d=delta, so0<=d<=P.
The constraint gives M(u^2+v^2)<=Au+d. Direct expansion yields

    A^2(T+d)^2-M T^2(Au+d)
       =A P T^2+(2A^2T-MT^2)d+A^2d^2>=0.              (16)

If the coefficient of d is nonnegative, every term is nonnegative.
If it is negative, substitute d<=P in just that term; the result is
at least P[(A-M)T^2+2A^2T]+A^2d^2>=0 by this regime's bound on M.
Dropping the second nonnegative radius square now proves the first
alternative A^2/(M^2T^2) in (15). No saturation or endpoint limit is
needed in this regime.

Suppose next M>A+2A^2/T. Then M>A. For fixed u,alpha put

    P=A-Mu, d=P-alpha, k=1+u, c=uP,
    x=v^2, c_0=u alpha/M, H_0=T+d.

Legal v>0 forces P>0. The ratio on the left of (15) is

    (u^2+x)/[H_0^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                  (17)

On x<=c_0 it increases. On x>=c_0, differentiation has the sign of

    H_0^2-2M^2c_0-M^2u^2
      +2M^2c_0^2/x+M^2u^2c_0^2/x^2.

The first three terms are at least

    T^2-2Mu(A-Mu)-M^2u^2
       =T^2-A^2+(A-Mu)^2>=0.

The branches agree at x=c_0. Therefore (17) is bounded by its value
at the allowed endpoint x=(d+c)/M. There
Mx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, giving the bound

    F_u(d)=(Au+d)/
       { M[(T+d)^2+M(1+u)^2d^2/(d+c)] }, 0<=d<=P.     (18)

This saturation is only an upper relaxation; no final activity is assumed.

Write b_0=Au. For u>0, c>0, differentiation of (18) has the sign of

    G_u(d)=(T+d)(T-d-2b_0)-M(1+u)^2 H_u(d),
    H_u(d)=d[(b_0+c)d+2b_0c]/(d+c)^2.

Explicitly

    H_u'(d)=2c^2(d+b_0)/(d+c)^3>=0,
    G_u'(d)=-2(d+b_0)-M(1+u)^2H_u'(d)<=0,
    G_u(P)=(T+A)(T-A-2Au).                            (19)

The last identity follows by substituting P=A-Mu and d+c=P(1+u):
the subtracted term at P is Mu(A+P+2Au), which combined with the
first product leaves exactly the displayed factorization.

In the present regime

    u<=A/M<T/(T+2A)<=(T-A)/(2A).

The last inequality is equivalent to (T-2A)(T+A)>=0. Hence
G_u(P)>=0. Because G_u is nonincreasing, G_u(d)>=G_u(P)>=0 throughout
[0,P]; thus F_u(d) is nondecreasing. When u=0, its formula reduces to
d/{M[(T+d)^2+Md]}, whose derivative has the sign T^2-d^2>0 for
0<d<=A. Its limit at d=0 is0. These observations also handle all
relaxed endpoint degeneracies; the actual constraint always has v>0.

It follows that (18) is at most its alpha=0 value at d=P. Writing
t=P in[0,A], that value is

    [A^2+(M-A)t]/[M^2(T^2+(2T+A+M)t)].                 (20)

This is a quotient of positive affine functions of t. Its derivative
has the sign of

    (T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)].

If nonpositive, the maximum is its value at t=0, namely A^2/(M^2T^2).
If nonnegative, the maximum is at t=A and equals
A/{M[(T+A)^2+MA]}. In the latter case

    M[(T+A)^2+MA]-A(T+A+M)^2
       =(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,

so the value at t=A is at most1/(T+A+M)^2. This proves (15).
Every relaxation, both radius branches, and the u=0 and boundary cases
have been retained. Combining (14) with (15) proves (6).

## 7. Every original ruler pair, with its actual tail price

Fix the ORIGINAL enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m, c_r=c_m and
rho=c_r-2, and put a_k=a c_r^k for the level-k checkpoint excess.
The exact mass recurrence and its solution are

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,
    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k.              (21)

The inequalities sqrt(2)<10/7 and m>=128 give

    12/5<lambda<c_r<49/20,
    0<a<lambda-2<rho<=9/20,
    M_k<=c_r^k, M_(k+1)<=c_r M_k, a_k<=rho M_k.         (22)

For the upper bound on c_r, use
c_r<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. For the bound on a, use
a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2. The remaining bounds follow
from the positive two-term formula (21). In particular the term
(a/rho)c_r^k alone proves a_k<=rho M_k.

A level-k checkpoint has an immediately adjacent H_k before and after
it. Its complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are therefore at least
M_k. For every first checkpoint in any pair, A/T<=rho<=9/20<1/2.
Thus T>=2A, as required by the new full-norm comparison.

For actual checkpoint indices i<j of levels k,l, respectively, put

    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2.

If i precedes the entire H_l immediately before j, then T>=M_k and
M>=M_l, so R_pair<=rho^2. This includes k>=l because such a level
cannot occur inside H_l. Otherwise i lies inside that particular H_l,
so k<l. Let x be1 plus i's local prefix mass inside H_l, and y=M.
Its local adjacent copies imply x,y>=M_k, and the exact identity is
x+y=M_l-a_k. As T>=x,

    TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k).

Write theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and zeta=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. The latter bound
follows from l>=k+1 and M_(k+1)=2M_k+a_k. Since E<=rho M_l,

    R_pair<=theta rho zeta/(zeta-1-theta)
           <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.

The middle function decreases in zeta for zeta>=2+theta, and the
last expression increases in theta>=0. This covers every actual pair.
In particular q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.

Palindromicity applies this very same pair estimate in reverse and
gives AE/(NM)<=q_pair. The local side bounds give A<=rho T and
E<=rho N. Consequently

    R_pair<=q_pair,
    T<=q_pair N/R_pair,
    M<=rho^2 N/R_pair.

All quantities divided by here are positive. The full physical word
mass, including both checkpoints and every ordinary query, is exactly

    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.

Multiplying by R_pair and using the preceding bounds yields

    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},
    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2.                         (23)

The constants satisfy

    q_pair D_pair<=2(29/20)(1/2)^2+(81/400)(1/2)
                  =661/800,
    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.

The actual M_m is at least16: its word has2^m-1 steps, each greater
than1. Therefore N_0=q_pair M_m/D_pair>1. For1<=N<=N_0 use (23)
and the increasing function N^2/(2N-1); for N>=N_0 use the other
branch and the decreasing function1/(2N-1). At their common crossover,

    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair).

Relative to the SAME-word canonical Huber value, this gives

    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]
       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)
       <=(661/800)*31/(32-199/60)=61473/68840.           (24)

The rational function decreases with M_m, so the final bound follows
from M_m>=16. The original tail factor2N-1 was retained throughout.

## 8. Complete SAME-word K, ray and applicable lens references

For accepted b98bb807d514e80d, EVERY single-cut term at a level-k step
h=1+a_k has denominator

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)], S_total=M_m-1,

where P is its COMPLETE physical prefix sum. Differentiation has the
sign of2(S_total-h)-3P, so its minimum between first and last occurrence
is at an endpoint. The first occurrence has prefix M_k-1 and suffix
at least M_(m-1)-1; for the last these are reversed. The first endpoint
is bounded using a_k<=rho M_k. For the other, use that x^2/(2x-1)
increases for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Thus every cut is at most
rho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Since M_m<=c_r M_(m-1),

    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)
                         <=3159/4000<gamma104.          (25)

Here (2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. Hence the COMPLETE maximum
K_n(H_m) is its canonical Huber term1/(2M_m-1), not a sampled cut value.

For the complete F_ray_n reference use precisely the accepted recurrence
and feasibility conditions of1fca3e5dd555cb4a. If a checkpoint selection
has at least two indices, its first two nonterminal magnitudes are
m_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM), and its old offset after the first cut is
O_1=(T-1)m_0. Its budget just after the second cut is

    E m_1-O_1=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,

because R_pair<=q_pair<1. With exactly two cuts, feasibility requires
this budget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more cuts, the next nonterminal
magnitude is this budget divided by a mass at least1. A nonpositive
budget already violates positivity; a positive budget still gives
m_2<m_0, violating the required repeated-ray magnitude condition.
Thus no selection with two or more checkpoints is feasible. Zero and
single checkpoint choices are exactly the complete K terms. We obtain

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).                  (26)

The second term of (6) is the second actual checkpoint's single-cut
term, because its full prefix mass plus1 is T+A+M and suffix factor
is2N-1. Apply (25) to it and (24) to the first term of (6). This proves
(7) uniformly over every feasible tuple and every original partition.

The complete applicable reference from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf is

    V_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}.

It contains this SAME Huber term by definition. Thus
V104<=gamma104 V_lens(H_m) for the entire family. This compares the
full Huber-augmented lens maximum without pretending to compute its
complex component. It does not equate the universal lens with the larger
word-dependent legal set D_h, and does not cover all nonlinear scalar
trajectories.

C59/accepted e9a55f57461f253c remains confined to its ACTUAL H6 with
epsilon1/6. Our m>=128 result is not a transfer of that witness to a
child word whose enclosing parameters changed. No new actual-H6 record
is asserted. Accepted62a7d9329121fd75 remains the finite(2,2,1)
calibration; it is not used as an iteration or family-optimality premise.

## 9. The original all-split B_n, without changing the denominator

From accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put C_n=1+S_circle(n),
B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1, and

    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),
    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

We give the sharper elementary upper estimate needed here, rather than
using an unverified asymptotic of the benchmark. Set p_0=log_2(lambda)>1
and f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. The second derivative of log f_0(t)
with respect to log t is

    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)
      [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.

The bracket is positive: it is0 at0 and has positive derivative for
t>0. Convexity at log t and-log t gives
f_0(t)f_0(1/t)>=f_0(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Consequently

    Omega(a,d)<=(a^(1/p_0)+d^(1/p_0))^p_0.

Indeed the proposed right side X>=max(a,d) obeys(X-a)(X-d)>=2ad,
where equality defines the positive root Omega; zero arguments follow
by continuity. Induction over ALL splits in the ORIGINAL recurrence
therefore gives C_n<=(n+1)^p_0. The inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d follows
by squaring sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a. A second all-split induction gives

    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                                     (27)

For an actual split k+l+1=n it uses (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0;
this follows by integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0 and
includes l=0. No reoptimized or selected denominator sequence appears.

The exact original enclosing-m mass in (21) satisfies

    M_m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m
      [a_m/(c_m-2)+(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] ->exp(1).

Here a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly bounded below1. Also
(2^m-1)^p_0/lambda^m->1. Combining (7) and (27) gives (8).
Its bound is strictly below1, since gamma104<1 and exp(1)>2. This
does not assert convergence of the actual family supremum.

## 10. What is decided, and what is not a legal inference

This joint draft decides the whole declared positive-quadrant endpoint
family, with no restriction on the acute angle, no prescribed coordinate
ratio, no cap on its magnitude, no linkage between alpha and beta, all
middle intercepts allowed by (2), and every original checkpoint pair.
It includes the orthogonal endpoint s=0. Its minimum set is the actual
tilted set(9), and the closest-minimizer normalization was optimized
exactly. Every physical query and every old-plane holding condition was
retained. It does not merely improve one orthogonal calibration.

The alpha=0 and v-square saturations in Section6 are upper relaxations,
not assertions that a chosen final gradient remains legal after those
changes. The only actual conversion claimed is the beta/intercept
conversion on0<=s<=1, explicitly proved in Section5. The all-positive
closure itself does not need that conversion, and does not silently
extend its legality to s>1.

Its exact remaining geometric escape is s<0, since then -2s alpha in
the terminal numerator is positive and the first inequality of Section5
does not follow. Such final gradients are obtuse to p, and (9)'s
coordinatewise closest-minimizer argument can still hold; the failure
is in the payoff charge, not automatically in the radius formula.
Negative ell, a changed sign pattern for q, nonzero first/final relative
affine costs, or more gradient changes/variation inside ordinary blocks
are also outside the declared family. C99/C100 are not universal
obstructions to any of these. No arbitrary-word minimax upper bound or
original-target theorem has been proved.

## 11. Actual obtuse static data outside the closure

To make the remaining s<0 hypothesis concrete, keep ANY actual
two-checkpoint word and the same T,M,N,A,E. Define the following new
parameters for this section only:

    B=E/2, D=M+B, v=sqrt(A/(4D)),
    alpha=min{A/2, (v/2)sqrt(B(2N+B))}>0,
    delta=A-alpha, beta=Bv, u=0, kappa=0,
    p=(1,0), q=(0,-v), z=(-alpha,Bv), r=z/(2N),
    J_obt(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>,<r,y>},
    F_obt(x)=min_y{J_obt(y)+||x-y||^2/2},
    X_0=(T+delta,-Dv).                                  (28)

Here p.r=-alpha/(2N)<0 and q,r are nonparallel. These are explicit
real vectors and a single function, not only a positive Gram matrix.
The direct regularity proof of Section2 applies verbatim to the finite
maximum in(28); none of its steps imposed a sign on r.

At the prefix ending query y=(delta,-Dv), p's height is delta>=A/2,
q's is Dv^2=A/4, r's is negative, and zero's is0. Earlier prefix
queries add a nonnegative multiple of p, strengthening each comparison.
At the middle ending query y=(-alpha,-Bv), q's height is Bv^2>0,
p's is-alpha<0, and its margin over r is

    [B(2N+B)v^2-alpha^2]/(2N)>0,

because alpha^2<=B(2N+B)v^2/4. Earlier middle queries add a
nonnegative multiple of q; its directional slope v^2 exceeds0,0,
and q.r=-Bv^2/(2N). Thus every middle query is legal. The second
checkpoint sends the point exactly to z.

At any tail mass t in[0,N-1], the proposed proximal point is

    y=[1-(1+t)/(2N)]z.

Its bracket lies in[1/2,1-1/(2N)] and is positive. The r-plane has
positive height, while p and q have negative heights. Hence every tail
query has gradient r. Identity(10) now proves every ordered row, with
zero cost for all slopes; no remote-row condition is omitted.

The actual minimizer is uniquely0. In fact p.y<=0 forces y_1<=0,
q.y<=0 forces y_2>=0, and r.y<=0 then forces both coordinates to0,
since both-alpha*y_1 and Bv*y_2 are nonnegative. Therefore

    R_obt^2=(T+A-alpha)^2+D^2v^2,
    V_obt=[(2N+1)/(4N^2)]
           [alpha^2+B^2v^2]/[(T+A-alpha)^2+D^2v^2].       (29)

The terminal affine reserve is||z||^2/(4N)>0. The first and middle
ending reserves are delta and Bv^2, also positive. All optimum slacks
are0, since every affine cost is0 and the true minimizer is0.

For appended total mass tau the proximal point is
[1/2-tau/(2N)]z. Every plane comparison holds exactly until tau=N.
At tau=N all four planes meet at0. For tau>N the r-plane is negative
and zero defeats it, so r cannot remain the gradient. The separate
zero,p,q support-plane limits all equal N: each margin at the terminal
point is N times its positive rate of loss under motion-r. Thus this
example's exact paid continuation is

    0<=tau<=N,
    V_obt(tau)=V_obt(0)-2tau||r||^2/R_obt^2.             (30)

This is a real static realization on EVERY original word and at its
actual n. It is not declared an improving adversary: the complete
same-word profile comparison of general obtuse endpoints remains OPEN.
The formulas alone are not that comparison. Its purpose is to certify
that the remaining negative-coordinate hypothesis has nonempty physical
content; the quantitative decided deliverable is the full positive
family domination(7)--(8), not an undecided optimization problem.

### 11.1. An original-word sign counterexample, with its weakness priced

Choose any two distinct level-0 checkpoints of the ORIGINAL H_m,
m>=128, in (28). Their excesses are A=E=a_m, so B=A/2 and
0<A<lambda-2<3/7<1/2. The ordinary blocks and all enclosing-m
parameters stay exactly as prescribed. At this legal static instance,
the difference between the two sides of the FIRST sign-deletion
inequality of Section5, before its square completion, is

    -2s alpha-2ell beta=(alpha^2-B^2v^2)/N>0.           (31)

To prove strict positivity, alpha^2 is the minimum of
A^2/4 and v^2 B(2N+B)/4. The first exceeds B^2v^2 because
4D>A. The second exceeds B^2v^2 because2N>3B. Both are strict from
D>=1,N>=1 and A<3/7. Thus dropping the assumption s>=0 in that
display really makes it false on original physical words. This does
NOT refute the full bound(15), whose hypotheses do not mention s,
or prove that the larger family beats the benchmark.

Indeed this very counterexample is weaker than the complete same-word
references. From D>=M>=1, alpha<=A/2 and A<1/2,

    alpha^2+B^2v^2<=A^2/4+A^3/(16D)<=9A^2/32,
    R_obt^2>=T^2+AM/4,
    V_obt<=27A^2/(128N R_obt^2).

Since M_m=T+M+N+2A, each term is bounded separately to give

    M_m/(N R_obt^2)<=2+4/A+2A.

For example the middle-mass term is at most4/(NA)<=4/A, the prefix
and tail terms are each at most1, and the excess term is at most2A.
It follows that

    (2M_m-1)V_obt
       <=(27/32)(A^3+A^2+2A)
       <=(27/32)(384/343)=324/343<1.                    (32)

The polynomial increases for A>=0, and its value at A=3/7 is384/343.
By (26), this proves V_obt<=(324/343)K_n(H_m)
=(324/343)F_ray_n(H_m), and the Huber-augmented lens also dominates.
By (27) and the original mass limit, its original-B limsup is at most
2(324/343)/exp(1)<1. This is a same-word counterexample to one proposed
proof shortcut, explicitly NOT an improving adversary. General obtuse
endpoint parameters and other checkpoint levels are not closed by (32).

## 12. Literature grounding and resource accounting

The search program was active-program/mutation: alter endpoint angle
within the known finite affine-envelope regime, with the missing
mechanism a full-gradient true-radius estimate on the original word.
Matlas returned arXiv2205.12838 Lemma5.8,2105.13913 Proposition3.1,
and2111.07720 Lemma5.1, all concerning constrained hull/Frank--Wolfe
geometry. Their compact-domain conditional-gradient assumptions do not
supply this unconstrained long-step payoff comparison; none was imported.
The targeted obtuse-example follow-up returned arXiv2111.14069 Lemma6
(negative-curvature saddle escape, incompatible with our convex setting)
and2112.02525 Theorem3.3 (a generic constrained stationarity statement).
Neither supplies or is used in the original-word counterexample.

Web retrieval recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods",arXiv1502.05666. The known exact local file
downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read at Section2.5,
Theorem4,its proof/construction,and Corollary1. Its finite Euclidean
smooth-convex definition specializes to smoothness1 here; the needed
regularity and all directed inequalities were rederived directly above,
with accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixing the project row convention.
No new external theorem is a proof dependency. This direction remains
inside that previously audited finite-envelope regime and needs no
additional machinery or unexamined external definition.

All mathematics was symbolic/textual. No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, formal verifier, installation, consult, native
subagent or new fact was used. The empty initial memory query returned
count0; the exact shared master_guidance file and targeted searches
recovered the steering. One long recall output was truncated; the
relevant files were subsequently read in bounded sections. These are
persistence/retrieval events, not mathematical verification.
One append patch was rejected with "invalid hunk" because an added line
lacked its patch marker; no partial edit occurred. The corrected patch
succeeded and the assembled proof was read back before publication.
